# ADR — STYNX 1.1.1 campaign control contract

- **Status:** Accepted for preparation; publication remains separately unauthorized
- **Date:** 2026-08-24
- **Authority:** Architect, implementing the human Owner's exact DEVAI 1.2.13 authorization
- **Baseline:** `b77b50230e3906cee632eb9218b06603cce6c89a`
- **Tree:** `d0cde059f7a93a624ff01d485ebda9ac3cb9422e`
- **Amends:** `ADR-VERSION-LINE-0001`, `2026-08-16-trusted-local-rc-evidence.md`
- **Related:** `2026-05-19-test-gate-tiers.md`, `2026-05-21-mutation-thresholds-tiered.md`, `2026-08-24-ci-economy.md`

## Context

The changeset release PR merged into `main` projected the root and all 44 publishable manifests to
`1.0.0`. That projection conflicts with the Owner's exact unified `1.1.1` decision and is not a
candidate, authorization, release receipt, or registry history. It must be reconciled through the
one-time governed rebaseline; it must not be adopted merely because it is now on `main`.

At this exact baseline deterministic discovery yields 44 publishable packages and 38 mutation
targets. Authenticated registry observation yields 38 existing private packages and exactly six
approved absent names: `@stynx-nyx/jobs`, `@stynx-nyx/mobile-runtime`,
`@stynx-nyx/notifications`, `@stynx-nyx/offline-sync`, `@stynx-nyx/outbox`, and
`@stynx-nyx/worklist`. Version `1.1.1` is absent from all 44 histories. These observations must be
recomputed at each frozen candidate; the machine-readable entry snapshot is
`law/policy/release-campaign-1.1.1.json`.

## Decisions

### D1 — Exact release unit and version

The release unit is the deterministically discovered 44-package roster. The one unified candidate
is exactly `1.1.1` for the root, every publishable manifest, every internal published STYNX range,
all create-app material, changelogs, changeset state, SBOM material, drafts, tarballs, and evidence.
The mutation roster is independently discovered and is exactly 38 at this baseline. Neither count
is a permanent shortcut: discovery and policy must agree, and any drift invalidates evidence.

Only the six names above may be treated as first publications. Every other publishable package must
exist, remain private, and reconcile with authenticated registry metadata. An unexpected absence,
presence, visibility change, candidate collision, partial response, malformed response, timeout,
or unauthenticated response fails closed.

The sole history exception is `@stynx-nyx/angular-profile@2.0.0`, usable only to permit candidate
`1.1.1` through registry monotonicity. It authorizes no deletion, deprecation, manual tag action, or
version substitution. `2.0.0` remains immutable non-canonical history unless the Owner separately
authorizes a precisely named later action.

#### D1.1 — PR A intentionally carries no Changeset

PR A is the governed campaign-preparation change, not a package-version candidate. It must carry
no package Changeset, including no empty Changeset added merely to make `changeset status` green.
PR B performs the one-time unified exact `1.1.1` rebaseline for the complete live roster and
materializes the corresponding manifests, changelogs, internal ranges, changeset state, SBOM,
drafts, tarballs, and evidence together.

The PR A release-status gate must therefore recognize only the exact campaign-preparation context
bound to this policy and its declared baseline, emit an empty non-promoting status projection, and
state that version projection is deferred to PR B. The exception must fail closed for an ordinary
package change, another baseline, another target version, a changed roster, any added or deleted
Changeset, or any `ci: version packages` commit. It does not authorize omitting Changesets from
ordinary development. Engineer owns the bounded deterministic classifier; Inspector must retain a
negative sensor proving that ordinary changed packages without Changesets still fail.

### D2 — Exact DEVAI 1.2.13 adoption

This decision supersedes only the prior campaign's exact DEVAI 1.2.12 preparation dependency. The
supported dependency target is exact `@aarusso-nyx/devai@1.2.13` with this authenticated identity:

- tarball: `https://npm.pkg.github.com/download/@aarusso-nyx/devai/1.2.13/6e766187269db2e5f494786adc7c00c62acad006`;
- integrity: `sha512-QM9PyGNtPRzhRBsYTiJFg6eM3OVw4/fSyAPnn1h8yHBq20GcEBrp91wxzqlvGREGkOAzezwvK3INze2OjqZV3Q==`;
- SHA-1: `6e766187269db2e5f494786adc7c00c62acad006`;
- SHA-256: `47f07c9ea4eecfb06c9ee6d0ec01ea46e3e76231e4c6151ddbde73ada26122d0`;
- source commit: `5df84ca88179a8fe53cc29cf8201c32c9439d552`;
- source tree: `36b5c714e8da19b5f42fb7db57d8fe9783c99b4f`;
- signed tag object: `32c9b98603d49fcabbf1db220b2f7438e0a40fc6`.

DEVAI 1.2.13 preserves the Doctor adopter-policy precedence correction and replaces the broken
adopter-checkout verifier lookup with an authenticated, immutable package materializer. Doctor
must validate and deterministically recompute the accepted materialization from the authenticated
installed package defaults plus `law/policy/devai-adoption.json`; it must not replace the adopter's
`COVERAGE`, `ERROR`, `FLOW`, `PRIVACY`, or `RBAC` domains with raw package defaults or trust receipt
hashes alone.

#### D2.1 — Supported trusted-local-RC verifier materialization

The installed 1.2.13 package is the materializer provider. Its canonical policy is
`dist/law/policy/trusted-local-rc-verifier-package.json`, SHA-256
`9990ce69fee0bca529b6337334a403cb569a200ad508c00b08d9e60b563696a0`. That policy deliberately
keeps the independently materialized verifier distribution at exact `@aarusso-nyx/devai@1.2.12`;
the provider package and verifier package are separate identities and must not be conflated. The
exact nested contract is recorded in `law/policy/release-campaign-1.1.1.json` and validated by
`law/schemas/release-campaign-1.1.1.schema.json`.

The generated workflow must use only the step-scoped `PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN`, with `contents: read`,
`packages: read`, and `checks: write`. It must not fall back to `github.token`, execute package
lifecycle scripts, execute candidate product code, or source verifier files from the adopter
checkout. Before extraction it must authenticate and verify the exact verifier package name,
version, tarball URL, SHA-1, SRI, release-source commit and release-source tree, and reject absolute,
traversing, symlink, hardlink, or special archive entries.

After extraction it must accept only `dist/runtime/evidence-verification`, provenance SHA-256
`e6be4198ded731b9733c8e5c720fc5beb1ec8393a1eda01b76e6119b172d17c0`, embedded source commit
`9e115014f8da5a16be526c7da5207bc0aae0801b`, all 21 declared payload files with exact digests, and
the five declared evidence binaries. Missing, extra, symlinked, special, digest-drifted, or
binary-map-drifted content fails closed. `DEVAI_LEDGER_VERIFIER_PROVENANCE_SHA256` is a required
external duplicate of the committed provenance digest, never the sole trust root. Missing package
authentication or a missing/mismatched external duplicate is blocking and has no fallback.

Engineer must pin the exact registry package and use supported `devai init bind`, `devai init
apply`, or documented installed evidence commands for generated `.devai` material. Hand editing
generated `.devai/pin`, `.devai/config`, `.devai/state`, or proof material is prohibited. Any
generated diff must be classified and reviewed; package installation alone is not adoption proof.
The installed version, lockfile resolution, registry, tarball, integrity, shasum, source identity,
binding receipt, recomputed materialization, and Doctor result must all agree. No other DEVAI
version is authorized.

### D3 — Coverage policy executes; it is not retired

The F3 decision is **execute**. The coverage policy, `INV-COVERAGE-001`, its threshold resolver,
and coverage configuration remain accepted and must not be deleted or weakened for this campaign.
Every applicable publishable workspace must expose an executable coverage command, root
orchestration must discover the live roster, and the authoritative run must emit a per-package
four-metric result. The configured thresholds remain the setpoints; an uncovered package or
unreachable threshold is a real blocking observation, not permission to lower a tier, exclude code,
suppress a package, or edit tests to move the number. Any future retirement requires a separate
Architect decision that updates the invariant, trace, readiness claims, and all consumers together.

Inspector owns coverage sensors and weakening analysis. Engineer owns package scripts,
orchestration, and CI wiring. Neither may silently change the accepted threshold semantics.

#### D3.1 — Coverage population follows executable semantics, not filenames

The shared `src/index.ts` and `src/**/index.ts` exclusions are not normative. An entry module that
contains runtime declarations, initializers, control flow, I/O, or callable implementation is part
of the coverage population even when it also exports the public API. A filename convention may not
remove executable plant from measurement. A pure barrel whose content is limited to import/export
wiring may be excluded only by a narrow, reviewable classification; the classification must fail
closed if executable behavior is later added. Likewise, `types.ts` may be excluded only while it is
limited to TypeScript-erased declarations and type-only imports or exports. Runtime enums,
constants, classes, schemas, initializers, or other emitted values in a types module are
coverage-bearing.

At this checkpoint, `packages/feature-flags/src/index.ts` and
`packages/integration-adapter/src/index.ts` contain their packages' executable implementation and
must be included. Their current `src/types.ts` files are type-only and may remain excluded. The
packages do not require a product refactor merely to make coverage non-empty. Moving behavior out
of an entry module is permissible only as an independently justified Engineer design change, and
all destination implementation modules remain in the measured population.

Engineer must remove the blanket index exclusions or replace them with an equivalently fail-closed
pure-barrel classifier, and must remove the two package-local zero-threshold overrides so the
accepted resolver supplies all four thresholds. Any narrow source exclusion must identify only a
mechanically verified non-executable module and must have a negative sensor proving that adding
runtime behavior makes it coverage-bearing. Inspector may strengthen tests against the existing
public behavior, but neither test additions nor source reorganization may reduce the measured
population, thresholds, assertions, or package roster. The authoritative 44-package run remains
blocking until every package has a non-empty four-metric population and satisfies its configured
setpoints.

### D4 — Generated README dependency truth

Consumer-facing package README dependency claims must be derived from current manifests and the
deterministically discovered workspace dependency graph, never maintained as independent prose.
The Engineer mechanism must have `--write` and non-mutating `--check` modes, stable package
ordering, canonical range rendering, and byte-stable reruns. It must cover all 44 publishable
packages and distinguish runtime dependencies, optional dependencies, peer dependencies, and
development-only relationships. Internal STYNX ranges in generated README sections must agree with
the candidate policy. Missing markers, duplicate generated sections, undocumented runtime/peer
dependencies, stale ranges, or hand-edited generated content fail the release-policy gate.

### D5 — API and trace completeness

Every publishable package must have one public API baseline discovered from its manifest exports
and built declarations. The baseline roster must exactly equal the 44-package release roster;
missing `jobs`, `notifications`, and `outbox` baselines are publication blockers. Unexpected
exports, missing export targets, declaration digest drift, or a baseline generated from an unbuilt
or different tree fails closed.

Trace verification must derive counts from tracked paths and reconcile all four populations:
tracked test paths, executable tests, fixture/support paths, and supplemental governed scripts or
config attestations. At the current PR A preparation tree, the live census is 495 tracked paths,
380 executable tests, 115 support paths, and 14 supplemental paths, totaling 509 governed items.
This supersedes the earlier 481/366/115/14 = 495 observation because Inspector strengthening added
nine executable sensors and the jobs contract closure added three executable tests without deleting
or reclassifying an existing path. The final local-RC blocker contract and notifications
mutation-survivor sensors add two executable tests without changing the support population. All 380
executable tests must map to active invariants with current assertion projections; all active
readiness-bearing invariants must have executable sensors. An unmapped test or assertion drift is
blocking.
Hard-coded stale counts may be updated only together with a complete regenerated trace mapping;
they must never be changed merely to silence the verifier.

### D6 — RLS and sourcemap observations fail closed

RLS verification is readiness-bearing only when it connects to the migrated PostgreSQL state and
executes its cross-tenant assertions before reset, as required by CI-economy Decision 6a. Missing
database configuration, unavailable PostgreSQL, inability to authenticate, zero discovered RLS
targets, or any skipped assertion is a failure to observe and therefore blocks the lane. A
developer diagnostic may report an explicit non-promoting skip, but a release, exact-main,
local-RC, or hardening profile must request live mode and cannot convert absence into success.

Sourcemap verification is readiness-bearing only when it inspects a non-empty expected population.
Before package assembly it must inspect the complete built Angular roster; after assembly it must
inspect every expected Angular tarball and map. Zero maps, missing build outputs, missing tarballs,
missing `sourcesContent`, unresolved sources, malformed maps, or roster mismatch fails closed.

### D7 — Hardening and k6

An explicit hardening dispatch with `scenario=all` must reach and successfully execute every k6
scenario. Registry authentication and DEVAI installation are prerequisites to the observation, not
substitutes for it. A 401, missing token file, dependency failure, quiescence skip, absent result,
zero summary files, failed threshold, missing comparison baseline outside the one explicit
first-seed case, cancellation, or timeout does not establish hardening evidence. The successful
workflow must bind run ID, commit, tree, scenario set, summary artifacts, and baseline identity.

### D8 — Branch and tag protection drift

Repository policy requires exact live-state observation before PR A merge and again before release.
The required contexts declared in code must equal the active branch-protection contexts; code-owner
review and conversation resolution must operate as declared; administrator enforcement must not
allow the candidate to bypass required checks. Release tag refs require a separate matching server
rule. A declarative Terraform file, local hook, or historical observation is not live enforcement.

This ADR authorizes read-only drift verification and fail-closed reporting only. Changing branch
protection, tag rules, required contexts, review policy, or bypass actors remains a separate Owner
external-action decision. PR A must not merge through an observed bypass.

PR A may be pushed solely to obtain exact-tip check-run identities while live settings still
drift, because an unpushed commit cannot have authoritative GitHub check runs. Push does not make
the candidate merge-ready. Before merge, the repository policy record and live `main` protection
must agree, every required context must be confirmed byte-for-byte from a successful check run on
the exact PR tip, and the release-tag ruleset must be active. A later commit invalidates those
exact-tip observations. No administrator or ruleset bypass may be used to merge or release.

### D9 — Fail-closed partial-publication recovery

Multi-package publication is not atomic. Before dispatch, the governed workflow must persist the
exact ordered 44-package plan and expected candidate artifact identities. After each attempted
package it must preserve an authenticated receipt containing package, version, outcome, integrity,
shasum, timestamp, workflow run, candidate commit, and candidate tree. Receipt persistence must not
expose credentials.

On the first ambiguous or failed outcome the workflow must stop initiating new publications and
must not automatically retry. Recovery begins with a fresh authenticated census of all 44 names and
classifies each as verified published, verified absent, collision, or unknown. Unknown is blocking.
Already published immutable `1.1.1` versions are never republished, deleted, deprecated, retagged,
or replaced. Preflight must recognize the recorded partial state rather than hiding it behind the
ordinary candidate-collision error. Resuming only the exact verified-absent suffix requires a new
Owner authorization naming the same candidate commit/tree, the exact published set, the exact
remaining set, their receipts, and the recovery command. Any tree, roster, artifact, or policy drift
requires a new campaign plan; no final success claim is permitted until all 44 artifacts and normal
`latest=1.1.1` outcomes independently verify.

### D10 — Local-RC E2E has one build producer and verified declaration outputs

The failed `9b14b6103ed73e796bda9f91c747fc7f429bfad2` local-RC observation does not
show a missing workspace dependency or a missing Turbo edge. `reference/api/package.json`
declares `@stynx-nyx/preferences` as `workspace:*`, and the resolved Turbo graph orders
`@stynx-nyx/preferences#build` before `@stynx-nyx/reference-api#build`. The race exists because
the root E2E population also starts `@stynx-nyx/reference-web#test:e2e`; its Playwright web-server
helper independently invokes `pnpm --filter @stynx-nyx/reference-api... build`. The two top-level
build populations can therefore run the destructive `@stynx-nyx/preferences` build concurrently.
One producer may remove or rewrite `packages/preferences/dist` after Turbo has marked its other
producer complete but before the Turbo-owned reference-api compiler resolves the package's
declarations. A dependency edge within either build population cannot order work in the other
population. The `test:e2e` script-local preferences build is also too late to protect
`@stynx-nyx/reference-api#build`, because Turbo runs that build before the test task body.

For one root `pnpm test:e2e` invocation, the deterministic contract is:

1. the Turbo task graph is the sole owner of prerequisite builds;
2. `@stynx-nyx/preferences#build` has exactly one live producer and completes before
   `@stynx-nyx/reference-api#build` starts;
3. successful preferences build output includes both
   `packages/preferences/dist/preferences/src/index.js` and
   `packages/preferences/dist/preferences/src/index.d.ts`, matching its manifest exports;
4. the reference-api build fails closed before compilation if either output is absent or does not
   resolve through `@stynx-nyx/preferences`;
5. E2E task bodies and Playwright web-server helpers consume already-built outputs and never start
   a nested build, delete a dependency output, or rewrite it during the consumer window; and
6. a forced clean-output run must recreate the outputs through the graph. Generated output remains
   ephemeral and uncommitted.

Engineer owns the smallest implementation that establishes that contract in `package.json`,
`turbo.json`, `reference/api/package.json`, `reference/web/package.json`,
`reference/web/playwright.config.mjs`, `reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`, and,
only if required for a fail-closed output check, an Engineer-owned script under `scripts/`.
`packages/preferences/package.json` may change only if the output declaration itself is proven
incorrect; preferences runtime source is not a remediation target. The implementation must not
serialize unrelated E2E work or use timing as coordination.

Inspector owns a regression sensor at `test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. It must
prove the resolved task edge, reject a nested reference-api or preferences build in every E2E task
body and web-server helper, verify both exact preferences outputs from a clean start, and prove
that reference-api compilation fails closed when the declaration output is missing. The sensor
must use process completion and exact output checks, never sleeps or cached/pre-existing output.

### D11 — Notifications mutation diagnosis classifies the report before subprocess prose

The first notifications failure is not evidence that its score is below `90`. The current wrapper
handles any non-zero Stryker exit before reading the canonical JSON report and sanitizes combined
subprocess prose as one string. Stryker may emit an absolute workstation path in otherwise
non-evidentiary clear-text output for either a genuine threshold exit or a harness failure. The
path rejection is correct, but that ordering discards the report that could distinguish the two
cases and leaves the score unknown.

The isolated diagnostic and the eventual wrapper behavior must use
`buildMutationEnvironment(process.env)` with `STRYKER_INCREMENTAL=false`, verify that registry,
GitHub, signing, and unrelated credential variables are absent from the child environment, and run
only `@stynx-nyx/notifications`. Raw stdout, stderr, JSON, HTML, temporary directories, and
workstation paths are private transient inputs: they are never printed, committed, copied into
evidence, or retained after classification.

Classification is deterministic and ordered:

1. if a JSON report exists, normalize it with the unchanged repository-root replacement,
   credential rejection, host-path rejection, roster, target, and threshold contracts, then derive
   exact `Killed`, `Timeout`, `Survived`, `NoCoverage`, total scored mutants, and score;
2. `score < 90` is `mutation-score-failure`, even when non-evidentiary subprocess prose also
   contained a workstation path; the exact counts and score are the only portable diagnostic;
3. a normalized report with `score >= 90` plus a non-zero exit, signal, spawn error, missing
   required result, or rejected subprocess prose is `mutation-harness-failure`, never a score
   failure;
4. a zero exit whose report cannot pass unchanged normalization is
   `mutation-portability-failure`; and
5. no usable report plus a non-zero exit is `mutation-harness-failure` with only a bounded
   credential-free reason code. It carries no score claim.

Engineer owns `scripts/run-mutation-evidence.mjs` and `scripts/lib/mutation-evidence.mjs` for this
classification/cleanup behavior. Engineer may touch `packages/notifications/src/**` only if the
isolated normalized report proves a score below `90` and a demonstrated plant correction is
needed; no other package plant is in scope. Inspector owns the diagnostic regression cases in
`test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs` and may strengthen existing notifications tests
under `packages/notifications/test/**` only after an exact below-floor report identifies surviving
behavior. The Inspector sensor must cover below-floor-with-path, at-or-above-floor-with-nonzero,
missing-report, unsafe-report, credential stripping, transient cleanup on every exit, and the
unchanged `break: 90`, mutate population, and roster.

### D12 — Package clean operations preserve the tracked tree

The final verification of candidate `547b77a045e803020b22a6c3b2d15a00827504a5`, tree
`66f63467123e145cad53b3c27e58b55077854ee8`, exposed two distinct clean-contract violations.
`@stynx-nyx/outbox` declares `pnpm exec rimraf dist coverage` without a resolvable declared
`rimraf` dependency and exits `254`; `@stynx-nyx/sdk` declares `src/generated` as a clean target
even though that directory contains tracked source. A failed executable and deletion of tracked
source are both hard failures. Neither may be hidden by skipping the package, narrowing the
publishable roster, installing an ambient tool, regenerating after deletion, or restoring files
after the clean command.

Every package in the deterministically discovered 44-package publishable roster must declare one
clean operation. For each operation, all of the following are required:

1. its executable is the declared Node runtime, the supported host `rm` command already used by the
   package-clean population, or a binary resolved from the exact frozen dependency graph;
   undeclared package executables, downloaded-on-demand tools, and workstation-only additions are
   not valid inputs;
2. every removal target resolves inside that package and is an untracked disposable output;
3. the only currently approved package-local removal targets are `dist` and, where produced,
   `coverage`;
4. every tracked path, including generated source committed under
   `packages-web/sdk/src/generated/**`, remains present with identical bytes and mode after clean;
5. a missing output is idempotent success, while an unresolved executable, target escape,
   symlink escape, tracked-path change, non-zero exit, signal, or incomplete 44-package execution
   fails closed; and
6. clean evidence is established in an isolated temporary copy. Verification must not clean,
   restore, or otherwise mutate the shared candidate checkout.

Engineer scope is exactly the `clean` fields in `packages/outbox/package.json` and
`packages-web/sdk/package.json`. Outbox must replace the unavailable `rimraf` invocation with the
declared Node 24 runtime and `node:fs` removal of only `dist` and `coverage`. SDK must remove
`src/generated` from its clean targets and retain cleanup of only `dist` and `coverage`. No
dependency or lockfile change, generated-source rewrite, root cleaner change, other manifest
change, source change, or test change is authorized.

Inspector scope is exactly `test/scripts/release-version-policy.test.mjs`. Its regression sensor
must discover the policy roster rather than use a hand-maintained package list; reconcile exactly
44 unique publishable manifests and 44 declared clean operations; prove every command uses
resolvable frozen tooling and names only approved package-local outputs; execute every clean
operation exactly once in a unique temporary repository fixture seeded with disposable outputs;
and compare the complete tracked-path byte-and-mode projection before and after. The sensor must
prove both idempotence and cleanup of the seeded outputs, preserve the shared checkout, remove its
fixture on every exit, and report any absent package, duplicate, unresolved tool, non-zero exit,
target escape, tracked-path drift, or incomplete population as failure. It must add no skip,
`todo`, `only`, sleep, exclusion, or post-clean restoration. Because the existing executable-test
path is strengthened, the 495/380/115/14/509 census remains unchanged; an Architect rebinds only
its actual assertion count and digest after the Inspector checkpoint.

Checks run cheapest-first: static 44-manifest census, clean-field and target validation, and tool
resolution; isolated fixture execution with tracked-tree comparison; the affected release-policy
sensor; the full default-concurrent script-test population; then official trace verification after
the assertion projection is rebound. Stop immediately on failure or an unexpected changed path;
do not run another mutation roster or PREPARE while this contract remains open.

### D13 — Privacy test tiers have disjoint execution populations

The forced-zero-cache verification of candidate
`343a94080ef4eb6ba5e4418c34446b0020c55ceb`, tree
`a1ce2e67e9bc86c1882c12f052f3c9b134812154`, timed out while the default-concurrent
`pnpm test` Turbo population executed
`packages/privacy/test/integration/privacy.module.spec.ts`. The failed observation spent
`111.69s` collecting and reached the existing `120000ms` test budget without an assertion,
open-handle, or teardown failure. One exact focused reproduction in the same environment passed
the integration case in `11.931s` with `2.39s` collection. That focused pass is diagnostic,
not readiness evidence.

The privacy integration sensor is selected twice: `packages/privacy/vitest.config.ts` includes
`test/**/*.spec.ts`, while `packages/privacy/vitest.int.config.ts` includes
`test/integration/**/*.spec.ts`. Trace classifies this file as suite `int`. Its execution in
the concurrent ordinary-test population is therefore a sensor-population error, not evidence of a
privacy plant failure.

The deterministic privacy population contract is:

1. the ordinary `test` population contains exactly the three unit specs
   `privacy-runtime.spec.ts`, `privacy.controller.spec.ts`, and
   `privacy.service.spec.ts`, plus the one `privacy-http.wiring-spec.ts`;
2. the serialized `test:int` population contains exactly
   `test/integration/privacy.module.spec.ts`;
3. the ordinary and integration populations are disjoint and their union is exactly all five
   existing privacy test files;
4. `test:coverage` continues to execute all five files through a dedicated
   `packages/privacy/vitest.coverage.config.ts`; this tier split may not reduce the established
   coverage population or thresholds;
5. every test file and assertion, the integration test's existing `120000ms` timeout, mutation
   population and thresholds, serialized integration tier, and default concurrency for unrelated
   tasks remain unchanged; and
6. the resolved populations must be derived from the frozen configuration on every verification.
   Cached or previously observed lists are not evidence.

Inspector scope is exactly `packages/privacy/vitest.config.ts`, the new
`packages/privacy/vitest.coverage.config.ts`, and
`test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. The ordinary config must select only the
three unit and one wiring files. The dedicated coverage config must preserve the exact prior
five-file population. The regression sensor must resolve all three populations, prove the exact
counts, membership, disjoint ordinary/integration intersection, complete five-file union, coverage
equality, unchanged timeout, unchanged root `test:int --concurrency=1`, and unchanged unrelated
default Turbo concurrency.

Engineer scope is exactly the `test:coverage` field of `packages/privacy/package.json`, which
must select `vitest.coverage.config.ts`. The existing `test` and `test:int` fields, root
scripts, `turbo.json`, privacy plant, test sources, and every other manifest field are outside
scope.

Checks run cheapest-first: static manifest/config and five-file census; resolved ordinary,
integration, and coverage lists; the affected blocker-contract sensor; privacy ordinary tests;
isolated privacy integration; privacy coverage with unchanged thresholds; the full
default-concurrent script-test population; then official trace verification after any required
assertion rebind. The 495/380/115/14/509 census and privacy test mappings remain unchanged. If the
existing blocker-contract sensor changes, an Architect rebinds only its actual assertion count and
digest. Stop immediately on an unexpected path, population, timeout, threshold, or trace change.

For D10 through D13, sleeps, committed outputs, sanitizer weakening, exclusions, target narrowing,
threshold changes, test weakening, and secret or workstation-path exposure are prohibited.
For D13 specifically, retries, timeout increases, global serialization, cache dependence, privacy
plant changes, and omission of any of the five tests from its governed unit/integration union or
coverage population are also prohibited.

### D14 — Reference-web E2E owns an isolated dynamic Redis endpoint

The consumed PREPARE observation of candidate
`fac9c19b0baa2f62fd63cc448fdfad20bf285608`, tree
`410fbaae99e85395dd91a4a0782555de6af68a89`, reached 46 of 48 forced-zero-cache E2E tasks before
`@stynx-nyx/reference-web#test:e2e` failed to start its temporary Redis service. The helper creates

defect, not an application assertion failure, and the protected stack is not a remediation target.

For each reference-web E2E stack invocation, the Redis endpoint contract is:

1. Redis retains container port `6379`, while Docker assigns the host port atomically; local Docker
   publishes through loopback as `127.0.0.1::6379`, and a declared
   `TESTCONTAINERS_HOST_OVERRIDE` uses the externally reachable binding established by the existing
   `test/support/redis-docker.ts` precedent;
2. only after the owned temporary Compose project reports successful startup, the helper queries
   only that project's `redis` service mapping for container port `6379`;
3. every non-empty returned mapping must parse as a valid numeric host port and all mappings must
   resolve to exactly one distinct port; an absent, malformed, out-of-range, or conflicting mapping
   fails closed;
4. reference-api receives a Redis URL constructed from
   `TESTCONTAINERS_HOST_OVERRIDE ?? 127.0.0.1` and the discovered port, never a fixed host port;
5. the helper must not pre-probe or reserve a purportedly free port, fall back to host port `6379`,
   connect to or reuse the protected Redis, or issue broad Docker discovery, stop, or removal
   commands; and
6. unique temporary Compose ownership and exact cleanup confinement remain unchanged. The
   remediation adds no sleep, retry, timeout increase, global serialization, PostgreSQL change,
   task exclusion, or task-graph change.

Engineer scope is exactly `reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`. Inspector scope
is exactly `test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. The Inspector sensor must prove

### D16 — Reference-api startup readiness is a credential-free event protocol

The focused D14 readiness observation of candidate
`d5bb384350bb5e40ec86bbada868afc46cc2f385`, tree
`21f0756fbed9e97c1f16da61a25ac429ee31e637`, proved the owned dynamic Redis contract: the unique
Compose project acquired `127.0.0.1:58087` for container port `6379`, its PostgreSQL and Redis
services were healthy, and protected-resource identities remained unchanged. The reference-web
E2E population then reached 30 of 31 forced-zero-cache Turbo tasks but timed out at the unchanged
Playwright web-server readiness bound before any browser assertion. The helper and API emitted no
bounded bootstrap phase or early-failure signal, so the observation cannot identify the stalled
pre-listen dependency. D14 succeeded; the new defect is an opaque readiness protocol, not evidence
for a Redis, PostgreSQL, storage, migration, or application assertion failure.

When `serve-reference-api-stack.mjs` owns the reference-api child, startup uses one
credential-free, event-driven IPC protocol. Its only API-emitted messages are fixed records with
protocol `stynx-reference-api-startup-v1` and these states:

1. `bootstrap-entered`, emitted immediately on entry to `bootstrap()`;
2. `nest-created`, emitted only after `NestFactory.create(...)` resolves and Nest initialization
   has completed;
3. `listening`, emitted only after `app.listen(...)` resolves; and
4. `bootstrap-failed`, carrying only one bounded reason code from
   `nest-initialization`, `pre-listen-configuration`, or `listen`.

No IPC record or related log may contain a raw error, stack, absolute or relative workstation
path, environment name or value, credential, connection string, URL, port, command line, or
arbitrary exception text. Outside the E2E-owned IPC launch, reference-api startup remains
compatible: an absent IPC channel neither adds a dependency nor prevents ordinary startup, while
its failure log is reduced to the same bounded reason code.

The helper must launch the compiled API with an IPC channel and register its `message`, `error`,
`disconnect`, and `exit` listeners before starting the existing cleanup watchdog. It accepts only
the exact protocol and ordered state transitions, records or prints only fixed phase/reason codes,
and treats an invalid record, `bootstrap-failed`, child error, IPC disconnect, or child exit before
`listening` as an immediate non-zero failure followed by the existing confined shutdown. It must
not wait for Playwright's outer readiness bound after such a terminal event. Receiving
`listening` proves only that `app.listen` resolved; Playwright's existing `/readyz` check remains
the live readiness sensor.

Engineer scope is exactly `reference/api/src/main.ts` and
`reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`. Inspector scope is exactly
`test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. No task, config, manifest, test population,
dynamic-port behavior, Compose ownership, protected resource, or unrelated startup behavior may
change. The remediation adds no timeout increase, delay, sleep, retry, polling, fallback, global
serialization, task exclusion, raw diagnostic output, or credential-bearing message.

The Inspector sensor must use credential-free fixtures to prove the exact ordered success
sequence; each bounded bootstrap-failure reason; invalid, duplicate, and out-of-order records;
listener installation before watchdog start; and immediate confined failure on child error,
disconnect, or exit before `listening`. It must also prove that ordinary non-IPC startup remains
supported and that neither the helper nor main source emits raw exceptions through this protocol.
Checks run cheapest-first: static path and protocol inspection; the focused blocker-contract
sensor; reference-api build; fixture startup success and every early-failure case; one isolated
live reference-web E2E run proving the three success phases, `/readyz`, assigned Redis host port,
and unchanged protected-resource identities; then, only after it passes, the complete
forced-zero-cache root E2E population and full default-concurrent script-test population. If the
existing sensor changes, an Architect rebinds only its independently verified assertion count and
digest; the 495/380/115/14/509 census remains unchanged.

Stop immediately on any unexpected path, missing/duplicate/out-of-order state, unbounded payload,
raw error or sensitive value, non-confined Docker operation, protected-resource interaction,
fixed-port fallback, timeout/retry/polling change, failed fixture/build/live check, or outer
readiness timeout. If the live run still reaches the unchanged outer timeout, its last fixed phase
is diagnostic only: preserve the worktree and evidence, make no readiness claim, and return to the
Owner before expanding scope. D15's second cycle starts only from the completed passing D16
lineage. A future candidate receives at most one PREPARE attempt, and only after D16, D15, the full
38-package mutation roster, and every cheaper prerequisite pass for that exact tree.

#### D16.1 — Governed startup codes use the Playwright-visible stderr channel

The second focused D16 continuation on candidate
`110a71340fe00e211db71cb5fd21fdaae579e2c8`, tree
`6cb1a11922d71f7d9a2cfffe932eb6a92760d9bc`, again reached the unchanged Playwright readiness
bound. The API success states were recorded by the helper on stdout, but the unchanged Playwright
web-server configuration does not report stdout by default; its visible Compose and timeout output
arrived on stderr. Consequently, absence of a visible success state does not establish that the
helper remained before child spawn, and the observation does not identify whether readiness
stopped in Compose, build-input verification, child launch, Nest initialization, listening, or
`/readyz`.

The helper must write every governed startup code to stderr and no governed startup code to
stdout. Every line is exactly `[reference-api-startup] <code>`, with no additional field or
payload. The success allowlist and order are exactly:

1. `helper-entered`, emitted synchronously immediately before invoking the existing Compose-up
   operation;
2. `compose-ready`, emitted only after that existing Compose-up operation resolves successfully;
3. `redis-mapping-resolved`, emitted only after the existing owned Redis mapping query returns one
   valid unique mapping;
4. `build-inputs-verified`, emitted only after the existing reference-api build-input verifier
   resolves successfully;
5. `child-spawned`, emitted only from the reference-api child's successful `spawn` event, never
   merely because the spawn call returned;
6. `bootstrap-entered`;
7. `nest-created`; and
8. `listening`.

Each success code is emitted exactly once. The five helper codes must precede the three existing
API codes, and no later code may appear until its named operation or event has completed. The only
governed terminal alternatives remain
`bootstrap-failed:nest-initialization`, `bootstrap-failed:pre-listen-configuration`,
`bootstrap-failed:listen`, `child-error`, `child-disconnect`, and `child-exit`; each is emitted at
most once on stderr at its already-authorized phase and triggers the existing immediate confined
shutdown. A terminal code forbids every later success code. Invalid, duplicate, out-of-order, or
unbounded IPC still fails closed and must not cause an unapproved diagnostic string to be printed.

Neither a governed line nor any related startup diagnostic may expose a raw record, error,
exception, stack, path, environment name or value, credential, connection string, URL, port, child
PID, command, or command argument. The implementation may change only
`reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`. `reference/api/src/main.ts`,
`reference/web/playwright.config.mjs`, every task and manifest, all ports and timeouts, and all D14
dynamic-port, Compose-ownership, cleanup, and protected-resource behavior must not change. No
delay, sleep, retry, polling, fallback, global serialization, or task exclusion is authorized.

Inspector changes are confined to `test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. Its
credential-free, Docker-free fixture must control each existing operation resolution and child
event independently, capture stdout and stderr separately, and prove the exact allowlist, ordering,
once-only behavior, operation boundaries, terminal cutoffs, and empty governed stdout. It must
prove that no code is emitted early; `child-spawned` requires the actual fixture `spawn` event; all
six terminal alternatives remain bounded and immediate; invalid input fails closed without raw
output; and the frozen main, Playwright, task, manifest, port, timeout, and D14 contracts do not
change. Existing tests and assertions may only be preserved or strengthened. If the sensor's
assertion projection changes, an Architect rebinds only its independently verified count and
digest; the 495/380/115/14/509 census remains unchanged.

Checks run cheapest-first: exact path and static stream/allowlist inspection; the focused blocker

default-concurrent script tests; any required Architect assertion rebind; and exactly one new

#### D17 — Post-listening readiness uses a bounded HTTP classifier

The focused D16.1 observation of candidate
`bd8bf774b37775c45c654f79f63e1fc8f015c09c`, tree
`6a5158aa48fff32f95672562e6d0810154685b5d`, emitted all eight governed success

dynamic Redis mapping rather than protected `6379`. Playwright nevertheless reached the unchanged
`/readyz` readiness bound. The configured probe and runtime both use loopback port `3000`, but the
preserved credential-free evidence contains neither a transport result nor an HTTP status.
Consequently, that observation establishes post-listening failure but does not distinguish an
unreachable listener from a readiness endpoint that returns non-success because a health
dependency is unavailable.

After receiving the exact existing `listening` code, a D17 diagnostic issues exactly one `GET
/healthz` followed by exactly one `GET /readyz` against the already-configured loopback endpoint.
The second request must not begin before the first request reaches its response or connection
terminal event. The classifier records only these fixed outcomes:

- `connect-failed` when either request cannot establish or retain the configured loopback HTTP
  connection;
- `healthz-2xx` or `healthz-non2xx` for the first response; and
- `readyz-2xx`, `readyz-404`, `readyz-503`, or `readyz-other` for the second response.

Only `readyz-503` may additionally record failing indicators from the exact existing built-in
allowlist `postgres`, `redis`, `jwks`, and `s3`, and only as indicator-name/pass-fail boolean pairs.
Unknown, duplicate, malformed, or non-boolean indicator material fails closed and is not retained.
The diagnostic must not retain or emit a response body, raw error, exception,
stack, request target, URL, port, filesystem path, environment name or value, credential,
connection string, header, command, argument, process identifier, or arbitrary dependency text.

Inspector changes are confined to `test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. Its
credential-free, Docker-free fixture must drive the existing `listening` event and each HTTP
response or connection terminal event independently. It must prove exactly-one request counts,
strict `/healthz`-then-`/readyz` ordering, the rule that `/readyz` cannot start before `/healthz`
terminates, every fixed classification, the allowlist and boolean-only `readyz-503` projection,
rejection of raw or unknown material, and immediate completion without a wall-clock sequencing
assumption. Existing assertions may only be preserved or strengthened. An assertion change
requires a later Architect rebind of only this sensor's independently verified count and digest;
the 495/380/115/14/509 census remains unchanged.

After the Docker-free fixture and all cheaper static, focused sensor, build, default-concurrent
script, trace, and strict-policy checks pass, Inspector has a budget of exactly one private,
credential-stripped diagnostic from the frozen candidate lineage. It may start only the existing
owned temporary reference stack and must use its existing dynamic mappings. It may neither
discover Docker resources broadly nor inspect, reuse, stop, reconfigure, or otherwise interact
with the protected reference stack. The normalized fixed classification is the only retainable
diagnostic result; raw response and transient diagnostic state must be discarded by the existing
confined callback cleanup, while the failed or diagnostic worktree itself remains preserved.

Interpret the diagnostic as follows: `healthz-2xx` followed by `readyz-503` demonstrates a
dependency-readiness failure but authorizes no correction until the exact allowlisted failing
indicator and its owning path return to the Owner; `connect-failed` demonstrates a transport
failure but likewise requires a separately authorized exact path; `readyz-2xx` contradicts the
preserved timeout and stops as non-reproducible; and every other sequence remains unclassified and
stops. No application, helper, health, Playwright, task, manifest, port, timeout, dependency, or
other implementation path may change under D17 itself.

Only after the Owner separately authorizes the exact demonstrated remediation, the responsible

and path gate passes may Inspector consume exactly one replacement focused reference-web E2E run.
That run retains all existing D14-D16 success, dynamic-port, protected-resource, phase-order,

#### D17.4 — The diagnostic builds the complete reference-api runtime closure

The D17.3 diagnostic from candidate
`b9f1c27c436e36597eb1432dd981dadac5e6022a`, tree

This was another diagnostic preflight omission, not a demonstrated product or environment defect.

paths, so a package-local reference-api build can compile while its emitted CommonJS still requires

that runtime and the other unbuilt package runtimes were absent, so module loading failed before

#### D17.5 — Compiled route metadata precedes any route correction

The complete D17.4 preflight on candidate
`511a00b10d5d206bcadabce44fa96eaeababdea4`, tree
`58b3e8fa449440f71b14ac49743e52152ae6dbe2`, was followed by one authoritative private
classification. The helper and API emitted all eight success phases exactly once and in order
through `listening`, then the classifier recorded exactly `healthz-non2xx` and `readyz-404`, made
one request to each route, retained no indicator or raw payload, completed its owned cleanup, left
protected resources unchanged, and left the candidate clean.

The repository contract is unambiguously root `GET /healthz` and root `GET /readyz` on the
existing loopback listener. `reference/api/src/main.ts` installs no global prefix, URI versioning,
router mapping, authentication middleware, or exception filter that can shadow those paths.
`StynxHealthModule.forRoot()` is present in `AppModule`; its root controller exposes unconditional
200 liveness and readiness that returns 200 or converts only `HealthCheckError` to 503. The
reference API supplies no readiness callbacks, so its built-in checks are skipped as up. Neither
method has an authentication guard. The global rate-limit guard passes handlers without
rate-limit metadata and cannot produce 404. The health wiring and integration sensors and the
source `AppModule` integration sensor already assert the same root contract. Consequently,
`readyz-404` proves that the health route was absent from the route table reached by the probe; it
is not a global-prefix, authentication, dependency-readiness, or ordinary status-transformation
failure. The coarser `healthz-non2xx` result is consistent with the same absence but does not by
itself establish its exact status.

Exactly two alternatives remain. Either the compiled package boundary lost or failed to
incorporate the health dynamic-module/controller route metadata even though D17.4 proved runtime
targets and compiled `AppModule` importability, or the queried listener exposed an absent or
different complete controller table despite the child reaching `listening`. No implementation
path is selected while those alternatives remain unresolved. In particular, adding an API-local
duplicate health controller is forbidden: it would mask the registration defect, risk duplicate
routes when the package controller is present, and change semantics without identifying the
failed boundary.

Inspector changes are confined to `test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. In the
existing direct-child Git-archive D17 fixture, after the exact D17.4 forced Turbo closure build, a
credential-stripped, Docker-free child must inspect only the compiled fixture modules and their
fixed Nest metadata. It must prove all of the following independently:

1. compiled `AppModule` metadata contains exactly one dynamic import whose `module` is the exact
   compiled `StynxHealthModule` resolved from the fixture reference API;
2. that dynamic import declares exactly one controller and it is the exact compiled
   `StynxHealthController` from the same resolved package bytes;
3. the controller path metadata normalizes to the root path;
4. the compiled `liveness` handler has request-method metadata `GET` and path metadata exactly
   `/healthz`;
5. the compiled `readiness` handler has request-method metadata `GET` and path metadata exactly
   `/readyz`;
6. no compiled or source bootstrap call enables a global prefix, URI versioning, or router mapping;
   and
7. the existing source and focused health/reference-api sensors continue to bind liveness to 200
   and readiness to 200 or 503, never 404.

The child emits only the ordered fixed classifications
`app-health-import-pass`, `health-controller-pass`, `health-root-pass`, `healthz-metadata-pass`,
`readyz-metadata-pass`, and `route-bootstrap-pass`, or the corresponding single `-fail` code at
the first failed assertion. A fail code terminates the child and forbids every later code. No code
may contain a raw metadata value, module or class name, response body, error, exception, stack,
path, environment name or value, credential, URL, port, command, argument, process identifier, or
arbitrary suffix. The parent retains only the fixed codes and pass/fail result. Every subprocess
working directory, build output, destructive target, and cleanup target remains confined to the
existing direct-child fixture; repository ignored outputs remain read-only and externally
inventoried. Existing D10 and D17.1-D17.4 assertions may only be preserved or strengthened.

A failed compiled assertion selects only its positively implicated later Engineer path:
`packages/health/src/health.module.ts` for dynamic-module/controller membership,
`packages/health/src/health.controller.ts` for controller or handler metadata, or
`reference/api/src/app.module.ts` for the compiled application import. The failure authorizes no
Engineer edit by itself; the Inspector returns the fixed failed assertion to the Owner for a new
role-separated correction dispatch. A passing metadata discriminator authorizes no product edit.
It permits only a separately dispatched, bounded private discriminator that makes exactly one
body-discarding request to each existing root health route, one existing public API-local route,

absent while the sentinel is 404, the remaining fault is listener or complete-route-table
identity. Any other sequence is unclassified and returns to the Owner. That later diagnostic has
no budget until the compiled discriminator passes and the Owner dispatches it explicitly.

#### D17.6 — The live adapter identifies its own governed route table

The D17.5 private discriminator on candidate
`f151787ff1a523d01b99a199848c9b2cffa3d2d3`, tree
`ef27ca0b44b6e6024485d50ed4c59717cf9559b7`, emitted all eight startup phases exactly once and in

`healthz-non2xx`, `readyz-404`, the statically selected public API-local route's exact absent class

import, and all six D17.5 route-metadata classifications had already passed. Cleanup completed,
protected resources remained unchanged, and no product correction was attempted.

That observation proves that the endpoint queried by the private discriminator exposed none of the
three governed GET routes `/healthz`, `/readyz`, and `/_reference/demo-tenants`. It does not prove
which process or adapter owned the queried socket: the private wrapper is not a repository-defined,
commit-bound sensor and retained no credential-free socket-to-child ownership receipt. Historical
process, socket, or ambient listener state is not candidate evidence. The root cause therefore
remains bounded unknown rather than a demonstrated empty Nest table or product route defect.

The remaining alternatives are ranked as follows:

1. the private wrapper queried a listener or endpoint other than the spawned child's live Nest
   adapter, including wrapper endpoint construction or address-family coexistence not represented
   by repository source;
2. the spawned child's live Nest adapter omitted all three governed routes despite the passing
   compiled module and route metadata; and
3. a repository host or port propagation mismatch is not selected because the helper passes its
   child port into the existing public loopback endpoint consistently and `app.listen` resolves
   only after Nest initialization and route registration.

The observed 404 fallback is a symptom of the table reached, not a separate cause. The selected
API-local route is enabled in the development configuration, public, and served from synchronous
in-memory state; it does not depend on Postgres or Redis. Global guards and interceptors cannot
explain the simultaneous absence of all three routes. No route, module, host binding, port, or
health behavior may change while the two live-identity alternatives remain unresolved.

After the existing `await app.listen(...)` resolves and after the existing `listening` phase is
emitted, `reference/api/src/main.ts` may inspect only its own live HTTP adapter's registered route
table for the exact GET routes `/healthz`, `/readyz`, and `/_reference/demo-tenants`. It must emit
through the already established child IPC channel exactly one of these payload-free codes:

- `runtime-route-table-present` when all three exact GET registrations are positively observed;
- `runtime-route-table-absent` when none of the three is observed; or
- `runtime-route-table-indeterminate` when the adapter cannot safely enumerate the table or when
  the result is partial, duplicated, ambiguous, or otherwise neither exact state above.

The code contains no route value, table entry, payload, count, error, exception, stack, address,
host, port, URL, path, environment name or value, credential, command, argument, process identifier,
or arbitrary suffix. Inspection must not invoke a route, open another listener, probe a socket, or
change registration. The existing startup and failure codes retain their order and meaning.

`reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs` may accept only those three exact fixed codes
from the existing child IPC channel. It prints the accepted code to the existing governed stderr
channel exactly once and only after `listening`. It fails closed with its existing confined cleanup
on an early, duplicate, unknown, malformed, or payload-bearing code, or if the child terminates
without exactly one accepted code. This acceptance is the only helper behavior change authorized;
host binding, endpoint construction, child launch, Docker, cleanup, phase sequencing, and all D14
through D17 behavior remain unchanged.

Interpretation is deterministic. `runtime-route-table-present` followed by an external
`full-table-absent` result selects a listener, socket, endpoint, or private-wrapper identity defect
and forbids a product route correction. `runtime-route-table-absent` selects the live Nest
registration boundary and returns to the Owner before any exact module or adapter correction is
authorized. `runtime-route-table-indeterminate`, a partial or contradictory state, or any protocol
violation stops the campaign at this decision and authorizes no live rerun or implementation
expansion.

Role separation is exact. This ADR is the Architect checkpoint. Inspector may change only
`test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs` to create a Docker-free, event-driven red sensor
that proves the three outcomes, exact route-method set, post-`listening` order, once-only acceptance,
IPC-only transport, stderr projection, and rejection of early, duplicate, unknown, malformed, and
payload-bearing messages without retaining raw data. Engineer may then change only
`reference/api/src/main.ts` and `reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`. Architect may
later rebind only the affected `law/trace.json` assertion projection when independent count and
digest recomputation proves that it changed. No role may cross those paths.

#### D17.7 — The commit-bound helper classifies only its explicitly owned listener

The D17.6 verification on candidate
`51f6f91372fe96cdecd03e11b9d1786ff3780dc2`, tree
`950c6621d3d33a1733a3e8007c82e127290b052c`, proved
`runtime-route-table-present` from the spawned child's live Nest adapter. The separately retained
four-request observation nevertheless classified the queried endpoint as `full-table-absent`.
Under D17.6 this selects listener, socket, endpoint, or private-wrapper identity rather than a
product route defect. No route, controller, module, health behavior, or product correction is
authorized.

A private external wrapper is rejected as the continuing evidence boundary. Copying repository
endpoint values into an uncommitted wrapper would not bind the requests, listener ownership, or
classification implementation to the candidate and would permit the same omission to recur. The
commit-bound `reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs` is the sole D17.7 classifier
owner. A helper-only request is not sufficient, however, while the normal child uses
`app.listen(port)` without an explicit host: another address-family listener may coexist and answer
the helper's loopback request. D17.7 therefore permits one diagnostic-only binding seam in
`reference/api/src/main.ts`; it permits no change to the normal listener.

The only opt-in is the exact singleton CLI argument `--d17-owned-route-classifier`. An invocation
with no arguments is the normal Playwright/server mode and performs zero helper-originated HTTP
requests. No environment variable, inherited value, alternate spelling, positional value, or
implicit test mode may enable the classifier. An unknown, duplicate, combined, or additional
argument exits nonzero before temporary-directory creation, Docker, build verification, child
spawn, or any governed phase, without printing the rejected argument or other raw material.

Opt-in mode uses the fixed diagnostic loopback host `127.0.0.1` and dedicated port `33117`. These
values are implementation constants, never caller input and never diagnostic output. Before
forming the child environment, the helper removes any inherited
`STYNX_REFERENCE_API_OWNED_DIAGNOSTIC` value. Only the exact CLI opt-in sets that internal child
control to `1`, passes `PORT=33117`, and retains the same fixed host and port for its own requests.
The API may test only that exact internal value: in diagnostic mode it calls
`app.listen(port, '127.0.0.1')`; otherwise its existing `await app.listen(port);` statement and
behavior remain byte-identical. Removing the bounded D17.7 diagnostic branch must reconstruct the
accepted D17.6 `main.ts` bytes exactly. There is no port preprobe, OS-assigned fallback, alternate
host, second bind, retry, or collision recovery. A failed diagnostic bind emits only the existing
bounded listen failure and performs zero HTTP requests.

The default helper accepts and prints the D17.6 runtime-table state exactly as before, starts no
classifier, and remains available for its existing Playwright lifecycle. In opt-in mode, only an
exact, once-only, post-`listening` `runtime-route-table-present` starts the owned classifier.
`runtime-route-table-absent` or `runtime-route-table-indeterminate`, an early, duplicate, malformed,
unknown, or payload-bearing record, or a child terminal before `present` performs zero HTTP
requests, invokes the existing confined cleanup exactly once, and exits nonzero.

After `runtime-route-table-present`, the helper issues exactly four sequential one-shot GET
attempts, in this order: `/healthz`, `/readyz`, `/_reference/demo-tenants`, and one fixed
guaranteed-absent sentinel. The next attempt begins only after the preceding response-headers or
connection-terminal event. Each slot is attempted exactly once even when its connection fails;
child termination or helper shutdown remains an immediate fail-closed terminal and invalidates an
incomplete four-attempt observation. The helper neither reads, parses, buffers, retains, nor emits
a response body. It discards the response stream and retains only one fixed classification per
slot:

- health: `owned-healthz-2xx`, `owned-healthz-404`, `owned-healthz-other`, or
  `owned-healthz-connect-failed`;
- readiness: `owned-readyz-2xx`, `owned-readyz-404`, `owned-readyz-503`,
  `owned-readyz-other`, or `owned-readyz-connect-failed`;
- API-local: `owned-api-local-2xx`, `owned-api-local-404`, `owned-api-local-other`, or
  `owned-api-local-connect-failed`; and
- sentinel: `owned-sentinel-404`, `owned-sentinel-other`, or
  `owned-sentinel-connect-failed`.

After all four slots terminate, the helper emits exactly one final table code.
`owned-full-table-present` requires health 2xx, readiness 2xx or 503, API-local 2xx, and sentinel 404. `owned-full-table-absent` requires exact 404 classifications for health, readiness, API-local,
and sentinel. Every other complete combination yields `owned-full-table-indeterminate`. The fixed
slot codes followed by the one final code are emitted once and in order through a dedicated
governed stderr prefix. Standard output remains empty. No output, retained state, or thrown message
may contain a response body, header, raw status, request target, URL, host, port, route, filesystem
path, environment name or value, credential, connection string, error, exception, stack, command,
argument, process identifier, adapter entry, or arbitrary suffix.

The opt-in helper performs its existing owned API and temporary Compose cleanup exactly once after
the final code or any failure. It retains no raw diagnostic file or transient report. A `present`
result closes the product-route question and classifies the prior external observation as rejected
wrapper/listener evidence. An `absent` result selects an owned live-dispatch contradiction but
authorizes no route change; it returns to the Owner for a new exact boundary. `indeterminate`, any
connection classification, an incomplete sequence, or any protocol or cleanup failure stops D17.7
without another diagnostic budget.

The governed stderr prefix is exactly `[reference-api-default-endpoint]`. The health slot emits

`default-healthz-connect-failed`. The readiness slot emits exactly one of
`default-readyz-2xx`, `default-readyz-404`, `default-readyz-503`, `default-readyz-other`, or

`default-endpoint-unavailable` only for health 2xx followed by readiness 503 with a valid bounded

All non-503 response bodies are left unread by the classifier and discarded through the response

### D20 — Spawned Playwright readiness accepts the governed route-table phase

The private D19 classifier from candidate
`82605f7ccb852cddde4971ebcc64e3daf75079a6`, tree
`3279f2fa7b80dfd181769612050ae2d9bdebc160`, used the unchanged default helper endpoint and
returned exactly `default-healthz-2xx`, `default-readyz-2xx`, and
`default-endpoint-ready`. A later focused forced reference-web E2E nevertheless reached the
unchanged `300000ms` Playwright web-server timeout after all eight startup phases and
`runtime-route-table-present` appeared exactly once; no Playwright test began. The endpoint was
classified as a direct loopback request with no proxy. This closes the application, route,
readiness-status, helper-owned binding, and proxy alternatives. The remaining defect is the
Playwright URL-readiness adapter for a helper process whose independently governed endpoint is
already proven healthy.

The candidate lock resolves exact `@playwright/test`, `playwright`, and `playwright-core` 1.60.0.
That implementation checks URL availability before spawning the configured command. When the URL
is already available and `reuseExistingServer` is true, it does not spawn a child and readiness
remains established only by that URL observation. When it starts the command and a `wait.stderr`
expression is configured, it creates a stderr wait promise and races process exit, the existing URL
availability loop, and the stderr expression under the existing web-server timeout. Either the URL
or the fixed stderr phase may therefore establish readiness for the spawned process without
weakening the preexisting-server boundary.

The Playwright 1.60 collector receives each raw child-stderr chunk and appends it to its private
matching buffer. Reporter prefixing is a separate projection of the same chunk and does not alter
the bytes evaluated by `wait.stderr`. Named capture groups alone are copied into `process.env` by
Playwright. The D20 expression has no capture group, so it creates no environment binding. Existing
D16-D19 controls already require helper stderr to contain only fixed governed lines and prohibit
raw errors, paths, environment material, URLs, ports, commands, arguments, credentials, or
arbitrary payloads. D20 adds no new output or exposure surface.

Engineer may add exactly one field to only the reference-api entry in
`reference/web/playwright.config.mjs`:

```js
wait: { stderr: /^\[reference-api-startup\] runtime-route-table-present$/m },
```

The regular expression is exact. It has beginning and end anchors, only the `m` flag, escaped
literal brackets, no wildcard, no alternation, no capture or named capture, and no `g` or other
flag. It matches the raw fixed helper line only. There is no `wait.stdout`, dynamic expression,
caller input, environment expansion, reporter prefix, fallback expression, second phase, or
application-specific payload.

The same reference-api entry must otherwise preserve byte-for-byte its command
`node scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`, current working directory expression, URL
`http://127.0.0.1:3000/readyz`, `reuseExistingServer: true`, and timeout `300000ms`. The fake-OIDC
and static-web entries remain byte-identical and gain no wait expression. Playwright configuration
outside this one field, package and root manifests, lockfile, tasks, ports, timeouts, proxy state,
and every D14-D19 input remain unchanged.

For a preexisting healthy URL, Playwright retains its existing URL-only reuse path and never waits
for stderr from a process it did not spawn. For a spawned helper, the existing URL observation and
the exact fixed stderr line race under the same timeout. The governed line is emitted by the helper
only after the child reports `listening` and the child's live adapter reports all three exact
governed routes present. The helper accepts that IPC state only once, after the complete ordered
startup sequence, and rejects early, duplicate, unknown, malformed, or payload-bearing records.
Main emits the state only after its listener and route-table inspection. D19 independently binds
that same default endpoint to health and readiness 2xx. These combined commit-bound controls make
the fixed phase sufficient for this campaign's spawned readiness without replacing the URL sensor
or authorizing a weaker general readiness contract.

Playwright resolves on the first valid race winner and does not itself defer for a hypothetical
later duplicate line. Inspector must therefore retain the existing main and helper proof that the
fixed state and stderr line are emitted exactly once and in order. Any weakening of that proof,
duplicate-capable producer, alternate prefix, or raw stderr path stops D20. No helper or runtime
change is authorized to accommodate the configuration.

Role separation is exhaustive. Architect owns this ADR and a later rebind of only the actually
changed assertion projection in `law/trace.json`. Inspector may change only
`test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. Engineer may change only
`reference/web/playwright.config.mjs`. `reference/api/src/main.ts`,
`reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`, health and route code, tasks, manifests,

### D21 — Compose-up failures terminate with one bounded classification

The replacement focused D20 observation of candidate
`28d0256de383989dd264d5898214996c78a2271c`, tree
`b2a81ec96d0fc831a2a31e0636a0741dd039db4e`, exited after exactly
`helper-entered` and before `compose-ready`. At that boundary the helper is awaiting only its
existing `docker compose -f <owned-compose-file> up --wait postgres redis` child. The helper
suppresses that child's output and currently collapses a spawn error, non-zero exit, or signal into
an unclassified exit after confined cleanup. The observation therefore proves a Compose-up
failure boundary, but it cannot distinguish those three terminal mechanisms and does not justify
a port, service, Docker, timeout, task, Playwright, or application correction.

The Compose-up operation gains exactly three credential-free terminal classifications:

1. `compose-up-spawn-failed`, emitted only when the Compose-up child reports a spawn error;
2. `compose-up-exit-nonzero`, emitted only when that child exits with a non-zero numeric status
   and was not classified as a spawn failure or signal; and
3. `compose-up-signaled`, emitted only when that child terminates with a non-null signal and was
   not already classified as a spawn failure.

Each classification is written exactly once as
`[reference-api-startup] <classification>` on governed stderr before the existing confined
Compose-down and temporary-directory cleanup begins. It is terminal: `compose-ready` and every
later success phase are forbidden after it. A successful zero exit emits no D21 terminal and
continues to the existing once-only `compose-ready` phase. Competing or repeated child events
must select only the first valid terminal outcome, and cleanup remains exactly once even when a
later event arrives.

No D21 line or related diagnostic may include a payload, child output, raw error, stack, path,
environment name or value, command or argument, URL, address, port, credential, exit number,
signal name, service status, project name, or arbitrary text. Compose stdout and stderr remain
suppressed. D21 adds no output capture, parsing, status probe, Docker discovery, preprobe,
fallback, retry, polling, sleep, delay, or timeout change.

The Compose command and arguments, fresh temporary Compose-file and project construction,
`postgres` and `redis` services, host and container ports, inherited environment, dynamic Redis
mapping, child environment, Playwright configuration, task graph, manifests, dependencies,
timeouts, and confined cleanup remain byte-for-byte or behaviorally unchanged as applicable.
Every D14 through D20 isolation, phase, route-table, listener, classifier, readiness, protected-
resource, raw-output, and cleanup control remains binding. A D21 terminal is diagnostic evidence
only; it is not evidence for a PostgreSQL-port, Redis, image, health, daemon, resource, or product
cause and authorizes no correction to those surfaces.

assertion projection in `law/trace.json`. Inspector may change only
`test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. Engineer may change only
`reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`. No other law, test, source,
configuration, manifest, task, lockfile, or generated path may change.

### D22 — Helper-owned PostgreSQL uses an atomic dynamic host mapping

The D21 replacement observation closes the Compose-up failure. The governed local-RC policy digest
for `STYNX_POSTGRES_PORT` equals the literal value `55433`; immediately before the helper began,
a protected healthy PostgreSQL resource already owned host port `55433`; and the helper emitted
exactly `compose-up-exit-nonzero` after `helper-entered`. The helper's temporary Compose project
still published its owned PostgreSQL service on that inherited fixed host port. Project-name
isolation does not isolate host ports, so the fixed PostgreSQL publication collided before
`compose-ready`. The protected resource is valid external state and remains outside remediation
scope.

For every helper-owned reference-web E2E stack, PostgreSQL retains service name `postgres` and
container port `5432`, but Docker assigns its host port atomically. Without a declared
`TESTCONTAINERS_HOST_OVERRIDE`, Compose publishes `127.0.0.1::5432`. With that declared override,
the existing D14 reachability precedent applies: Compose publishes `0.0.0.0::5432`, while the
child connects through the declared override rather than through the wildcard mapping address.
The inherited `STYNX_POSTGRES_PORT` remains part of the governed environment but no longer selects
or reserves the helper-owned publication and is never a fallback.

Only after the existing owned Compose-up operation succeeds, the helper queries exactly
`docker compose -f <owned-compose-file> port postgres 5432`. It performs no project-wide or
daemon-wide discovery. Every non-empty mapping line must parse as one bracketed or unbracketed IP
address plus one base-10 port in the inclusive range 1 through 65535. In local-loopback mode the
only accepted mapping address is `127.0.0.1`; in declared host-override mode the only accepted
mapping addresses are `0.0.0.0` and `::`. Multiple address-family mappings are allowed only when
every line is otherwise valid and all lines resolve to one identical port. An absent line,
hostname, unexpected IP address, malformed bracket or delimiter, non-decimal or out-of-range port,
or conflicting port fails closed before build-input verification or child spawn.

The reference-api child receives the PostgreSQL endpoint host
`TESTCONTAINERS_HOST_OVERRIDE ?? 127.0.0.1` and the single discovered port in each of its existing
Owner, application, and reader database URLs. Those three bindings retain their existing scheme,
database name, username, and password and differ only by replacing the fixed inherited host port
with the owned discovered port. The mapping text, selected host, selected port, and resulting URLs
must never be emitted. No other child environment binding changes.

Engineer scope is exactly `reference/web/scripts/serve-reference-api-stack.mjs`. The correction
changes only the generated PostgreSQL host publication, the owned `postgres/5432` mapping query
and parser, and the three child database URL port inputs. The Compose command and arguments,
temporary project and file ownership, services, container ports, PostgreSQL credentials and
healthcheck, Redis generation and D14 mapping, inherited environment, startup codes and D21
terminal classifications, reference-api child command, Playwright configuration, task graph,
manifests, dependencies, timeouts, watchdog, shutdown, and confined cleanup remain unchanged.

Inspector scope is exactly `test/scripts/local-rc-blocker-contract.test.mjs`. Its Docker-free,
event-driven sensor must prove:

1. local and declared-host-override PostgreSQL publication generation with no fixed host port;
2. the exact owned `postgres/5432` mapping command and no broad Docker or protected-resource
   discovery;
3. acceptance of the exact address allowlists and one consistent valid port;
4. rejection of absent, hostname, unexpected-address, malformed, non-decimal, zero, out-of-range,
   and conflicting mappings before build verification or child spawn;
5. propagation of only the selected endpoint host and discovered port to all three child database
   URLs, with no inherited-port fallback or diagnostic exposure; and
6. byte or semantic preservation of the services, container ports, PostgreSQL credentials and
   healthcheck, Redis/D14 behavior, Compose command, environment, D16-D21 protocols, timeouts, and
   exactly-once confined cleanup.

The sensor uses only fixture-owned files and child events. It invokes no Docker, binds no listener,
issues no HTTP, mutates no shared ignored output, and uses no wall-clock timer, retry, sleep, or
polling. Existing tests and assertions may only be preserved or strengthened.

raw-output, and cleanup assertions; Inspector red; Engineer green; the complete blocker

Only after every cheap gate passes may Inspector consume exactly one fresh-worktree forced focused
